{"parentUuid":"3d37f00e-efbd-415d-9c19-d83575e117c9","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a1e1e6cc6e256fa2a","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- craftsmanship-init: Rigorously read a project codebase and emit a tailored craftsmanship-audit config. Detects languages, frameworks, existing tool configs, and exclusions, then calibrates adaptive thresholds (long_method_loc / large_class_loc / long_parameter_list) from the project's actual p90 metrics — never generic defaults. Smoke-tests required tooling (ruff/eslint/gocyclo/cargo) and fails hard with a precise reason on any gap. Multi-language (Python, TS/JS, Go, Rust). Use this BEFORE running the loop-craftsmanship-audit plugin so the audit is 100% aligned with the project's conventions.\n- file-issue: Abre um issue no tracker (GitHub/Linear/Jira) com o MÁXIMO de contexto acionável para o time resolver sem ida-e-volta. Use SEMPRE que for reportar um bug/achado — inclui dedup-check, repro numerado, evidência (console/rede/logs), causa-raiz provável, severidade, escopo/incerteza honesta e issues relacionados. Nunca inclui secrets. Sintaxe livre — descreva o achado.\n- framer: Use when the user wants to design, edit, or publish a website or web page — creating layouts, editing sections, updating text or images, managing CMS collections and content, syncing external data, creating or modifying code components, managing color and text styles, handling localization, or publishing deployments. Trigger when the user mentions Framer, references their website or web pages, asks to edit designs, update site content, or work with any Framer project — even if they don't explicitly say 'Framer'. **Mandatory precondition**: run `npx @framer/agent@latest setup` and let it complete **BEFORE** loading this skill.\n- framer-code-components: Framer code component implementation guidance, platform constraints, layout annotations, property controls, and authoring best practices. Use only after loading the framer skill and the generated framer-project-<projectId> skill first in the same task; never load this skill directly as the entry point.\n- analysis: PhD-level trajectory validation with empirical evidence — benchmarks, complexity metrics, architecture fitness, scalability projections. Opt-in per project via rules/analysis-config.txt. Runs 6 analysis modules (A1-A6) weighted by project profile (engine/api/library/cli/infrastructure). Produces hypothesis-driven report with quantitative evidence, not opinions. Independent cycle — does not gate the main chain.\n- ast-grep: Structural search and refactor across multi-language codebases via tree-sitter patterns. Use when you need queries Grep can't express — function signatures, class hierarchies, decorator + function, call sites, async patterns, type definitions. Especially useful inside knowledge-base/references/ during /discover-execute. Composable from any context.\n- auto-plan: End-to-end autonomous orchestrator for cycle-discover + cycle-plan + cycle-implement + cycle-code-quality + cycle-review + cycle-release. Single entry-point chains the whole pipeline from idea to a release PR awaiting human approval. Default is full-pipeline; --plan-only retains the legacy discover+plan behavior. Depth (none/light/full) is derived deterministically from a confidence score against repo state — no interactive prompts. MUST-FIX items from /edge-case-plan are auto-injected into the plan before /plan-confidence re-scores. Inspired by planning-with-files v2.43.0 autonomy + composes Claude Code primitives (/plan-goal, /plan-loop) absorbed 2026-05-26.\n- code-quality: Audit project code for dead symbols, fabricated APIs, cross-package orphans, and weak test quality across Go, Python, TypeScript, and Rust (per rules/code-quality-languages.txt enablement). Auto-detects manifests; runs knip + vulture + cargo-udeps + deadcode + tree-sitter symbol fabrication checks + ast-grep cross-package wiring + stryker/mutmut mutation testing. Read-only by design (never edits code). Use after /implement completes its halt-loop, before /review begins — or standalone for periodic audits.\n- deck: Create complete presentation decks with diagrams. Orchestrates /marp-slide and /excalidraw into a single workflow — plans structure, creates diagrams, builds slides, renders HTML + PPTX. Use when the user wants a full presentation with visuals.\n- deps-audit: Audit project dependencies for known vulnerabilities (CVEs) and outdated versions across npm, Python, Rust, Go. Auto-detects manifests; runs osv-scanner + npm audit + npm outdated + pip-audit + cargo audit + govulncheck; cross-references a plan's\n- discover-confidence: Score a blueprint produced by /discover-execute for structural quality (M2 deterministic check). Mirrors /plan-confidence but with a blueprint-shape rubric (research coverage, reference citations, completeness, smells).\n- discover-edge-cases: Analyzes a discovery plan and identifies unforeseen edge cases — focused on deep research over sources declared in the plan (local references under .claude/knowledge-base/references/ and/or allowlisted URLs). Pragmatic — flags real risks without complicating the plan. Use after /discover-plan or when reviewing any plan under .claude/knowledge-base/discoveries/plans/.\n- discover-execute: Executes a discovery plan via halt-loop (ralph-loop-style autonomous iteration) over the sources declared in the plan — local references under knowledge-base/references/ e/ou URLs allowlisted via rules/discover-web-allowlist.txt. Produces a technical blueprint at knowledge-base/discoveries/blueprints/{slug}-blueprint.md. Use after /discover-edge-cases has approved the plan.\n- discover-improve: Iteratively improve a blueprint's discover-confidence score by applying deterministic fixes + LLM-driven semantic fixes via a halt-loop (ralph-loop-style autonomous iteration). Mirrors /plan-improve but for blueprints.\n- discover-plan: Turn the current context + any reference projects under knowledge-base/references/ + allowlisted external sources into a discovery plan saved at knowledge-base/discoveries/plans/{slug}-plan.md. Use when you want to plan a deep-research investigation into a technology or pattern before producing a blueprint.\n- discover-plan-confidence: Score a discovery plan produced by /discover-plan for structural quality (M2 deterministic check). Sibling of /discover-confidence with a discovery-plan-shape rubric (research coverage, reference citations, plan completeness, smells). Use after /discover-edge-cases, before /discover-execute.\n- dogfood: Honesty gate that blocks any 'production-ready' / 'production-grade' / v1.0 claim without recorded evidence of sustained internal use. Reads the project's dogfood manifest at knowledge-base/dogfood/manifest.md plus evidence files at knowledge-base/dogfood/evidence/, applies the project-specific dogfood golden rule (rules/dogfood-golden-rule.md — created per project), and emits EVIDENCE_SUFFICIENT / EVIDENCE_WITH_CAVEATS / EVIDENCE_INSUFFICIENT. Invoke before any production claim, milestone promotion targeting v1.0, README/CHANGELOG edit that touches status language, or release decision.\n- edge-case-plan: Analyzes an implementation plan and identifies unforeseen edge cases. Pragmatic — flags real risks without complicating the design. Use after /to-plan or when reviewing any plan in knowledge-base/plans/.\n- excalidraw: Create Excalidraw diagram JSON files that make visual arguments. Use when the user wants to visualize workflows, architectures, or concepts (standalone or as input to /marp-slide and /deck).\n- frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- grill-me: Interview the user one question at a time until shared understanding on a plan/feature/design is reached. Walks the decision tree branch by branch, resolving dependencies between decisions. For every question, explores the codebase first (Grep/Read) if the answer is there; only asks the user when the answer requires intent/preference/business context. Persists the conversation to knowledge-base/grills/{slug}-grill.md as input for /to-plan. Use BEFORE /to-plan when the feature is non-trivial AND the user has not yet articulated requirements precisely, or when the user explicitly says \"grill me\". SKIP for trivial fixes or when /to-plan can already synthesize from context.\n- implement: Executes an implementation plan from cycle-plan via halt-loop (ralph-loop) with TDD discipline + wiring triad (caller + integration test + runtime metric) + quality gates (SOLID, Clean Code, DRY, Design Patterns). Single entry-point for cycle-implement. Use after /to-plan chain returned verdict ≥ SHIPPABLE_WITH_CAVEATS while working on `develop`.\n- implement-m0-walking-skeleton-sepa-knowledge: Domain knowledge skill paired with the SEPA agent for plan m0-walking-skeleton. Consult ALWAYS during /implement cycle when reasoning about TDD, SOLID, Clean Code, DRY, design patterns, OR wiring triad — this skill hydrates community best practices via WebSearch on top of plan-specific context (ADRs + edge-case findings + project rules). Triggering phrases: \"review this against community standards\", \"what's the canonical pattern\", \"is this idiomatic\", \"best practice for Ink terminal UI components, ink-testing-library snapshot determinism, ESM-only TypeScript library packaging, tsup dts build, terminal render benchmark\".\n- implement-m1-chat-surface-sepa-knowledge: Domain knowledge skill paired with the SEPA agent for plan m1-chat-surface. Consult ALWAYS during /implement cycle when reasoning about TDD, SOLID, Clean Code, DRY, design patterns, OR wiring triad — this skill hydrates community best practices via WebSearch on top of plan-specific context (ADRs + edge-case findings + project rules). Triggering phrases: \"review this against community standards\", \"what's the canonical pattern\", \"is this idiomatic\", \"best practice for Ink Static windowed chat thread, ink-testing-library stdin input tests, grapheme Intl.Segmenter text buffer, React memo streaming render, terminal chat composer\".\n- marp-slide: Create Marp presentation slides with embedded CSS theme — outputs self-contained .md + rendered .html + .pptx. Use when the user wants slides only (no full deck with diagrams; for that use /deck).\n- plan-confidence: Score a plan produced by /to-plan for structural quality (M2 deterministic check). Sibling of /discover-confidence with a plan-shape rubric. Use after /edge-case-plan, before /implement.\n- plan-help: Show all available Cycle ecosystem commands with descriptions and recommended flows. Use when the user asks for help, wants to know what commands are available, or says \"what can you do\" / \"help\" / \"list commands\".\n- plan-improve: Iteratively improve a /to-plan plan's M2 score by applying deterministic fixes + LLM-driven semantic fixes via a ralph-loop-style autonomous iteration. Use after /plan-confidence returns a verdict below SHIPPABLE_WITH_CAVEATS (NON_SHIPPABLE or INVALID) and you want the system to attempt auto-improvement before human intervention.\n- quality-init: Rigorously read a project codebase and emit tailored Claude Code quality-gate hooks (PostToolUse) that block code smells on every Write/Edit. Detects languages, frameworks, existing linter configs, and test directories, then calibrates adaptive thresholds (max_complexity / max_function_lines / max_nesting_depth / max_parameters / max_file_lines / duplicate_min_lines) from the project's actual p90 metrics — never generic defaults. Generates Python hook scripts + settings.json patch. Use this BEFORE starting any coding session where you want automatic quality enforcement via hooks.\n- release: Cuts a semver-tagged release from develop → main after /review returns READY_TO_MERGE. Auto-derives version from CHANGELOG sections (major/minor/patch), rewrites [Unreleased] under the new version header, commits chore(release), opens a PR develop→main with rendered release notes, and waits for human approval (the only manual gate — Unbreakable Rule 4). On merge, creates an annotated tag and a GitHub release. Single entry-point for cycle-release. Use after /review {slug} returned READY_TO_MERGE.\n- review: Most rigorous gate of the ecosystem. Validates quality gates + line-by-line plan vs implementation + 100% integration + integration test depth + edge-case coverage. Before starting, generates specialized review agents for the plan's domain (architecture, tests, wiring, cross-validation, domain-specific) and spawns them in parallel. Single entry-point for cycle-review. Use after /implement passed on `develop` and recent commits are ready to be audited (typically before a release cut).\n- review-m0-walking-skeleton-architecture-knowledge: Architecture-review paired knowledge skill (template)\n- review-m0-walking-skeleton-cross-validation-knowledge: Cross-validation paired knowledge skill (template)\n- review-m0-walking-skeleton-domain-frontend-knowledge: Domain-review paired knowledge skill (template)\n- review-m0-walking-skeleton-domain-testing-knowledge: Domain-review paired knowledge skill (template)\n- review-m0-walking-skeleton-tests-knowledge\n- review-m0-walking-skeleton-wiring-knowledge\n- roadmap-feature: Add a new feature to an existing ROADMAP.md as the next M<N+1> milestone — without renumbering anything that came before. Reads the existing roadmap, detects the next free milestone ID, runs a 4-question focused grill (why now / dependencies / DoD / risks), cross-checks against the existing \"Explicitly out of scope\" section, optionally clones additional SOTA references when the feature needs peers not already covered, and appends one new `## M<N+1> — [ ]` block to ROADMAP.md plus a `[Unreleased] § Added` entry to CHANGELOG.md. Sister skill of /roadmap-init — same isolation contract, opposite trigger (refuses if ROADMAP.md does NOT exist). Cap is open at M9, M10, M11… extend freely.\n- roadmap-init: Bootstrap a new project from a one-line idea into a macro ROADMAP.md + a curated set of SOTA reference repositories cloned under knowledge-base/references/. Runs a 7-question Socratic interview (problem → users → in-scope → out-of-scope → constraints → success criterion → north-star metric), then researches state-of-the-art peers via web search, gates them by license (warns on GPL/AGPL/BUSL/no-license), clones the approved ones with shallow blob-filter clones, and writes ROADMAP.md (M0-M8 cap, checkbox per milestone) at the repo root. Use ONLY at project inception, BEFORE any /grill-me, /discover-plan, /to-plan or /implement work. REFUSES to run if ROADMAP.md already exists.\n- skill-creator\n- to-plan: Turn the current conversation context into an implementation plan and save it to knowledge-base/plans/. Use when user wants to create a plan from the current context.\n- deep-research: Deep research harness — fan-out web searches, fetch sources, adversarially verify claims, synthesize a cited report. - When the user wants a deep, multi-source, fact-checked research report on any topic. BEFORE invoking, check if the question is specific enough to research directly — if underspecified (e.g., \"what car to buy\" without budget/use-case/region), ask 2-3 clarifying questions to narrow scope. claude-design:youtube-clipper\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by running the app and observing behavior. Use when asked to verify a PR, confirm a fix works, test a change manually, check that a feature works, or validate local changes before pushing.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo, defaults to 10m) - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; for your working diff use /code-review\n- identity (`===`) signals change.\n114\t- **live tail** — the last `windowSize + windowOverscan` messages that re-render (streaming zone).\n115\t- **static prefix** — older messages rendered ONCE through Ink `<Static>` into terminal scrollback.\n116\t- **streaming append-in-place** — caller replaces the LAST message object with a longer `content`.\n117\t- **grapheme** — user-perceived character (`Intl.Segmenter`); cursor ops never split emoji.\n118\t- **submit vs newline** — Enter submits; Ctrl+J (the literal `\\n` byte) inserts newline everywhere;\n119\t  Shift+Enter inserts newline only when the terminal encodes shift (kitty protocol).\n120\t\n121\t### Architecture boundaries affected\n122\t\n123\tPer `rules/architecture.md § 1-3`: `src/text-buffer.ts` is pure domain logic (no ink/react\n124\timports — unit-testable without TTY); Blueprint Corner 2 verdict: **ZERO new runtime dependencies**\n156\t> (grapheme handling via built-in `Intl.Segmenter`; ink provides Static/useInput/useFocus).\n157\t\n158\t### Existing — use as-is\n159\t\n160\t| Package | Version | Ecosystem | Why |\n161\t|---|---|---|---|\n162\t| `ink` | `^5.2.0` | npm | `Static`, `useInput`, `useFocus`, `Box/Text` — all M1 primitives (Blueprint §\"Coverage Corner 2 — Dependencies\") |\n163\t| `react` | `^18.0.0 \\|\\| ^19.0.0` (peer) | npm | memo/useReducer/useMemo |\n164\t| (devDeps unchanged) | — | npm | vitest/ink-testing-library/tsup/eslint/prettier/tsx as released in v0.1.0 |\n165\t\n166\t### New — to be introduced\n167\t\n168\t| Package | Version | Ecosystem | Rule 9 rationale (libs evaluated) | Why this one |\n169\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n170\t| (none) | — | — | Evaluated: `string-width` (rejected — Ink wraps text itself at M1), `clipboardy` (rejected — paste is out of M1 scope), `chalk` direct (rejected — ink `<Text inverse>` covers the cursor) | Blueprint Corner 2: analogs' thread/composer code needs no external runtime dep |\n171\t\n172\t### Removed\n173\t\n174\t| Package | Last version | Why removed |\n175\t|---|---|---|\n176\t| (none) | | |\n177\t\n178\t## ADRs\n179\t\n180\t> D1–D6 originate in the blueprint (Blueprint §\"ADRs\" carries the full evidence); restated here\n181\t> condensed and self-contained. D7+ are plan-local.\n182\t\n183\t### D1 — ChatThread: messages-prop API with windowed `<Static>` history (immutable prefix contract)\n184\t\n185\t**Decision:** `ChatThread({ messages, windowSize = 8, windowOverscan = 4 })` with\n186\t`messages: ChatThreadMessage[]` (`{ id: string; role: ChatRole; content: string }`). Prefix\n187\t(`length − windowSize − overscan`, clamped ≥ 0) renders through `<Static>` keyed by `id`; tail\n188\trenders live via a memoized row. Documented contract: messages that leave the live window are\n189\tFROZEN (append-only history).\n190\t\n191\t**Rationale:** Mirrors the react-ink windowing mechanics with an explicit-props API (M7 owns\n192\truntime state); gemini-cli proves Static history in production; Ink has no viewport — Static IS\n193\tscroll.\n194\t\n195\t**Alternatives considered:** No windowing by default (rejected: the M1 DoD names `<Static>` for\n196\thistory); semantic last-user-prompt boundary (rejected: role-policy in a primitive — YAGNI);\n197\tindex keys (rejected: caller data needs stable ids; react-ink can key by index only because its\n198\tstore is append-only).\n199\t\n200\t**Consequences:** Callers own message identity; benchmark data tunes the defaults.\n201\t\n202\t### D2 — Streaming contract: rows memoized by message object identity\n203\t\n204\t**Decision:** Row component wrapped in `memo` with comparator `prev.message === next.message`.\n205\tStreaming = caller replaces the LAST message object with a longer `content`; only that row\n206\tre-renders. No throttling at M1.\n207\t\n208\t**Rationale:** Identity comparison gives the react-ink memo bound without a store; bench\n209\tevidence shows memo+windowing cut mean/peak frame times vs legacy; 16ms token cadence needs no\n210\tthrottle.\n211\t\n212\t**Alternatives considered:** Per-row store subscriptions (rejected: M7); deep-equality memo\n213\t(rejected: O(content) per row per frame; identity IS the React data contract).\n214\t\n215\t**Consequences:** In-place mutation (same reference) does not repaint — documented and tested.\n216\t\n217\t### D3 — ChatComposer: uncontrolled grapheme-aware buffer; Enter submits, Ctrl+J newline\n218\t\n219\t**Decision:** `ChatComposer({ onSubmit, placeholder?, multiLine = true, autoFocus = true })`;\n220\tinternal reducer buffer `{ text, cursorOffset }` with grapheme-aware ops (insert,\n221\tdelete-backward, delete-forward, move-left, move-right, move-home, move-end, newline, clear)\n222\tvia `Intl.Segmenter`. Keymap: printable → insert; Enter → submit (whitespace-only → no-op);\n223\tCtrl+J → newline (reliable everywhere — it IS `\\n`); `key.shift && key.return` → newline when\n224\tthe terminal encodes it; arrows/home/end move; backspace/delete delete. Cursor = `<Text inverse>`\n225\ton the grapheme at cursor (space at EOL/newline). Buffer clears on submit.\n226\t\n227\t**Rationale:** Buffer shape + segmenter proven in react-ink `useTextBuffer`; keymap mirrors\n228\t`ComposerInput` with the honest Shift+Enter caveat (ink's `key` cannot distinguish it in legacy\n229\tterminals); Enter-sends is the chat convention (ink-ui evidence).\n230\t\n231\t**Alternatives considered:** Controlled-only value/onChange (rejected at M1: every caller\n232\tre-implements cursor state — controlled mode is additive later); gemini backslash-continuation\n233\t(deferred M2+); word-nav/kill ops (deferred — additive actions); `usePaste` (deferred M2+ —\n234\tseparate event channel).\n235\t\n236\t**Consequences:** Reducer is exported and test-first; TTY-free unit tests cover every op.\n237\t\n238\t### D4 — `system` role via theme tokens (additive extension)\n239\t\n240\t**Decision:** Role union becomes `\"user\" | \"assistant\" | \"system\"` (exported as `ChatRole`);\n241\t`defaultTheme.role.system = { glyph: \"· \", prefix: \"gray\", text: undefined }`;\n242\t`TheoThemeOverride.role.system` accepted; the guard message names the 3-role union.\n243\t\n244\t**Rationale:** M1 DoD names three roles; additive to v0.1.0 API; glyph keeps roles\n245\tdistinguishable in NO_COLOR (M0 review positive finding).\n246\t\n247\t**Alternatives considered:** Separate `SystemMessage` (rejected: M0 D4 chose one role-switched\n248\tprimitive); color-only distinction (rejected: breaks color-independent readability).\n249\t\n250\t**Consequences:** M0's invalid-role test message updates (CHANGELOG § Changed documents it).\n251\t\n252\t### D5 — Test strategy: pure-reducer units + real-stdin integration + rerender streaming sequences\n253\t\n254\t**Decision:** (1) `text-buffer` reducer: TTY-free unit tests per op incl. grapheme edges\n255\t(emoji); (2) composer: real `stdin.write` tests with ink's byte sequences (`\"\\r\"` Enter,\n256\t`\"\u001b[D\"` left-arrow, `\"\b\"`/`\"\"` backspace, `\"\\n\"` Ctrl+J) + one-tick settling;\n257\t(3) streaming: `rerender()` sequences asserting `lastFrame()` progression + a repaint-scope\n258\tunit test (memo spy counting row renders); (4) Static: assert `lastFrame()` contains history +\n259\ttail (Static output accumulates in frames); windowing boundary asserted by counting rendered\n260\trows.\n261\t\n262\t**Rationale:** Byte sequences are authoritative from ink's own test suite; Static-in-frames\n263\tbehavior verified in ink's tests; mirrors the analogs' idioms with our M0 determinism kit.\n264\t\n265\t**Alternatives considered:** Mocking Static (reserved for boundary unit test if frame-count\n266\tproves flaky); snapshot-only (rejected: interactive semantics need semantic asserts).\n267\t\n268\t**Consequences:** M0 kit (renderFrame, env pins, NO_COLOR subprocess probe) reused; the probe\n269\tgains a thread+system-role scene.\n270\t\n271\t### D6 — Bench: chat-thread mode matrix; NEW baseline file; M0 baseline untouched\n272\t\n273\t**Decision:** `benchmarks/chat-thread.bench.tsx` — 500 messages + 300 streamed tokens\n274\t(replace-last + append pattern), modes `plain` (windowSize ≥ length) vs `windowed` (8+4);\n275\tprotocol/env identical to M0 (1 warmup + 5 runs, mean ± std dev, pinned FORCE_COLOR); output\n276\t`docs/benchmarks/m1-chat-thread-baseline.json` (M0 schema + `mode` field per entry + per-mode\n277\taggregate). `--smoke` = 1 run per mode, no file write.\n278\t\n279\t**Rationale:** Extends the proven harness; plain-vs-windowed is the decision-relevant\n280\tcomparison (rows are memoized by design — a no-memo mode tests nothing we ship);\n281\t`rules/analysis-golden-rule.md § 3` statistical rigor; separate file preserves the M0\n282\tregression anchor.\n283\t\n284\t**Alternatives considered:** Overwrite M0 baseline (rejected: destroys regression anchor);\n285\t3-mode legacy comparison (rejected: legacy mode is not shippable code).\n286\t\n287\t**Consequences:** Windowing benefit is measured, not assumed; defaults revisited with data.\n288\t\n289\t### D7 — ChatThread validates message ids at the boundary (fail-fast on duplicates)\n290\t\n291\t**Decision:** `ChatThread` throws `TypeError` naming the duplicate `id` when `messages`\n292\tcontains duplicate ids (single `Set` pass per render, before any hook side effects beyond the\n293\tscan).\n294\t\n295\t**Rationale:** Duplicate keys inside `<Static>`/React silently corrupt the append-only\n296\twatermark and row identity — a silent-corruption class bug (`rules/error-handling.md § 1-2`:\n297\tvalidate at the boundary, fail fast with typed errors). O(n) over ids per render is negligible\n298\tagainst Ink's render cost (evidence: M0 bench 11.2ms/frame floor).\n299\t\n300\t**Alternatives considered:** Dev-only check (rejected: the lib has no dev/prod build split at\n301\tM1); silent last-wins dedup (rejected: hides caller bugs — fail-fast rule).\n302\t\n303\t**Consequences:** One more negative-case test; documented in JSDoc.\n304\t\n305\t### D8 — Interactive example degrades to a scripted demo when stdin is not a TTY\n306\t\n307\t**Decision:** `examples/chat.tsx` renders thread + composer with a fake streaming assistant\n308\treply; when `process.stdin.isTTY` is falsy it skips the composer (renders thread + streaming\n309\tonly) so `pnpm example:chat | cat` stays a valid non-TTY smoke.\n310\t\n311\t**Rationale:** ink's `useInput` requires raw-mode stdin; piped runs must not crash (roadmap\n312\tnon-TTY constraint; M0 example smoke precedent).\n313\t\n314\t**Alternatives considered:** TTY-only example (rejected: loses the CI-viable smoke); mocking\n315\tstdin in the example (rejected: examples are honest consumer code).\n316\t\n317\t**Consequences:** The example doubles as the wiring caller for thread+composer+streaming.\n318\t\n319\t## Drawbacks & Risks\n320\t\n321\t| Drawback / Risk | Severity | Mitigation | Owner |\n322\t|---|---|---|---|\n323\t| `<Static>` frozen-prefix contract can surprise callers who edit old messages | Medium | JSDoc contract + a test pinning \"edits to static prefix do not repaint\"; documented in CHANGELOG entry | implement |\n324\t| Shift+Enter newline silently unavailable in legacy terminals (only kitty encodes it) | Medium | Ctrl+J documented as the guaranteed newline; JSDoc caveat (blueprint Rec 8); behavior test asserts Ctrl+J path | implement |\n325\t| Real-stdin tests may flake if settling is insufficient after `stdin.write` | Medium | One-tick `renderFrame` discipline + explicit awaited ticks between writes (ink suite uses staggered timers); two consecutive full-suite runs before commit | implement |\n326\t| ChatThread windowing interacts with ink-testing-library frame accumulation (Static output accumulates) — assertions must target `lastFrame()` content, not frame counts | Low | D5 strategy asserts content presence + row-count via memo spy, not raw frame counts | implement |\n327\t| 500-msg benchmark wall-clock in CI smoke | Low | `--smoke` = 1 run per mode; measured full runs only local (M0 precedent) | implement |\n328\t| Example with fake streaming timer could leave a dangling interval on exit | Low | clear interval on completion + `useApp().exit()` when scripted demo ends | implement |\n329\t\n330\t## Unresolved Questions\n331\t\n332\t(none — every decision is resolved at plan time: blueprint ADRs D1–D6 + plan ADRs D7–D8; the\n333\tonly environment-sensitive behavior, Shift+Enter, ships with the documented Ctrl+J guarantee.)\n334\t\n335\t## Critical paths\n336\t\n337\tFor `/code-quality` D4 when enabled: `src/text-buffer.ts` (cursor/grapheme branching),\n338\t`src/chat-thread.tsx` (window split + duplicate-id guard), `src/chat-composer.tsx` (keymap\n339\tdispatch).\n340\t\n341\t## Dependency Graph\n342\t\n343\t```\n344\tPhase 1 (system role) ──▶ Phase 2 (ChatThread) ──▶ Phase 3 (ChatComposer) ──▶ Phase 4 (bench + example)\n345\t                                                                                     │\n346\t                                                                                     ▼\n347\t                                                                       Final Phase (integration validation)\n348\t```\n349\t\n350\tSequential: thread rows render ChatMessage (needs system role); the example composes\n351\tthread+composer; bench renders the thread. Composer's reducer (T3.1) is independent of Phase 2\n352\tbut kept in sequence for one-vertical-slice discipline.\n353\t\n354\t---\n355\t\n356\t## Phase 1: System role\n357\t\n358\t**Objective:** `ChatMessage` accepts `system`; role: ChatRole; content: string }\n478\tinterface ChatThreadProps { messages: ChatThreadMessage[]; windowSize?: number; windowOverscan?: number }\n479\tChatThread({ messages, windowSize = 8, windowOverscan = 4 }):\n480\t  assertUniqueIds(messages)                       -- D7: TypeError naming the duplicate id\n481\t  tailStart = max(0, messages.length - max(0,windowSize) - max(0,windowOverscan))\n482\t  prefix = messages.slice(0, tailStart)           -- <Static items={prefix}> keyed by m.id\n483\t  tail   = messages.slice(tailStart)              -- <MemoRow key={m.id} message={m}>\n484\t  render <>{Static when prefix.length>0}{<Box flexDirection=\"column\">tail rows</Box>}</>\n485\t\n486\tMemoRow = memo(({message}) => <ChatMessage role={message.role}>{message.content}</ChatMessage>,\n487\t               (prev, next) => prev.message === next.message)\n488\t```\n489\t\n490\t- Invariants: prefix rows are appended-only (Static watermark — never re-rendered); tail rows\n491\t  re-render ONLY when their message object identity changes (D2); empty `messages` renders\n492\t  nothing (no Static mount — avoids the ink#904 class of issues).\n493\t- Edge cases: `windowSize=0` (everything except overscan tail in Static); thread shorter than\n494\t  window (no Static at all); duplicate ids (TypeError); replace-last streaming (only last row\n495\t  repaints — proven by memo spy test in T2.2).\n496\t\n497\t#### Tasks\n498\t1. move/delete\n617\t  step by grapheme (emoji \"👍\" is ONE step); home/end = line boundaries within multi-line text\n618\t  (previous/next `\\n`), matching the analog's multiline semantics.\n619\t- Edge cases: cursor at 0 (delete-backward no-op), cursor at end (delete-forward no-op),\n620\t  insert at middle, newline at middle, empty text ops, emoji at cursor boundary.\n621\t\n622\t#### Pseudo-code / Signatures\n623\t```pseudocode\n624\tfunction textBufferReducer(state, action): TextBufferState\n625\t  case insert:  text.slice(0,cur) + action.text + text.slice(cur); cur += action.text.length\n626\t  case delete-backward: prevGrapheme(cur) → splice out; cur = prevStart\n627\t  case move-left/right: cur = prev/nextGraphemeBoundary\n628\t  case move-home/end:   cur = lineStart/lineEnd (nearest \"\\n\" boundaries)\n629\t  case newline: insert \"\\n\"\n630\t  case clear:   { text: \"\", cursorOffset: 0 }\n631\t\n632\t# Example\n633\tinput:  {text:\"héllo\", cursorOffset:5} + move-left ×2\n634\toutput: cursorOffset lands before \"l\" (grapheme steps, é = one step)\n635\t```\n636\t\n637\t#### Tasks\n638\t1. The main session may still proceed with explicit justification.\n\n## Context you have (verbatim — DO NOT summarize)\n\n### Plan\n```\n---\nslug: m1-chat-surface\nmilestone_id: M1\ncreated_at: 2026-07-06\ngoal: Ship the M1 chat surface (ChatThread with Static history, ChatComposer multi-line input, system role, streaming render) with all gates green in CI and a committed thread benchmark baseline.\n---\n\n# Plan: M1 Chat surface core — ChatThread + ChatComposer + streaming render\n\n> **Version 1.2** (v1.1 absorbed edge cases; v1.2 matrix task-mapping + concurrency escape normalization; /plan-confidence SHIPPABLE 93.2) (absorbs MUST FIX EC-1 + SHOULD TEST EC-2/EC-3 from\n> `reviews/m1-chat-surface-plan-edge-cases-2026-07-06.md`) — Implements `ROADMAP.md § M1` on top of the released v0.1.0 walking skeleton:\n> extends `ChatMessage` with the `system` role, ships `ChatThread` (windowed `<Static>` history +\n> memoized live tail, token-streaming friendly) and `ChatComposer` (grapheme-aware multi-line\n> input via a pure reducer + `useInput`), adds streaming render tests + real-stdin input tests,\n> an interactive example, and a mode-matrix thread benchmark with a committed baseline. Zero new\n> runtime dependencies. All design decisions locked by the m1-chat-surface blueprint\n> (SHIPPABLE 99.7).\n\n## Goal\n\nEnable TypeScript agent-CLI developers to render a streaming chat thread with history in\n`<Static>` and compose multi-line input from the built `@theokit/tui` package so that the M1\nchat surface is proven end-to-end, measured by the CI gate chain (format → lint → typecheck →\ntest → coverage → build → bench smoke) exiting 0 on `develop`.\n\n## Context\n\nv0.1.0 (M0) shipped `ChatMessage` (user/assistant), the theme stub, the five-gate toolchain and\nthe benchmark harness with a committed baseline. `ROADMAP.md § M1` now requires the chat surface\ncore: three roles, `ChatThread` with `<Static>` history, `ChatComposer` multi-line input, and\ntoken-by-token streaming render — with snapshot tests per role + a streaming sequence, readable\nin NO_COLOR. The DISCOVER cycle produced a SHIPPABLE blueprint (99.7) locking six ADRs from the\nreact-ink/gemini-cli/ink evidence: windowed Static history, identity-memo streaming contract,\ngrapheme-aware buffer, Enter/Ctrl+J keymap (Shift+Enter honored when the terminal encodes it),\nreal-stdin test strategy with ink's authoritative byte sequences, and a mode-matrix bench.\nRoadmap risks: viewport/height correctness (resolved: `<Static>` IS Ink's scroll mechanism —\nBlueprint §\"Q1\"); streaming re-render perf/flicker (resolved: memo rows + windowing —\nBlueprint §\"Q2\").\n\n## Baseline Context (deep review of current state)\n\n### Files that will be touched\n\n| File | LoC today | Last commit (sha + date) | Why it exists today | Invariants to preserve |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `src/chat-message.tsx` | 36 | `ba938f0` (2026-07-06) | M0 primitive — role glyph + themed colors | Public `ChatMessage({role, children})` keeps working for user/assistant; guard stays FIRST statement (EC-1 M0; react-hooks lint) |\n| `src/theme.tsx` | 92 | `04fb7e6` (2026-07-06) | Semantic tokens + provider + hook | `useTheoTheme()` never undefined; leaf-level merge; deep-frozen defaultTheme; memoized context value |\n| `src/index.ts` | 7 | `d1a3c0b` (2026-07-06) | Composition root (only public surface) | Existing exports unchanged; `VERSION === package.json.version` |\n| `src/chat-message.test.tsx` | 138 | `04fb7e6` (2026-07-06) | M0 tests incl. EC-1 typed-error + NO_COLOR subprocess | Invalid-role message assertion updates to the 3-role union (documented change) |\n| `src/theme.test.tsx` | 96 | `04fb7e6` (2026-07-06) | Theme tests (5) | All keep passing; system tokens extend defaults |\n| `src/chat-thread.tsx` (NEW) | 0 | — | ChatThread component | — |\n| `src/chat-thread.test.tsx` (NEW) | 0 | — | co-located tests | — |\n| `src/text-buffer.ts` (NEW) | 0 | — | pure grapheme-aware reducer | — |\n| `src/text-buffer.test.ts` (NEW) | 0 | — | co-located reducer tests | — |\n| `src/chat-composer.tsx` (NEW) | 0 | — | ChatComposer component | — |\n| `src/chat-composer.test.tsx` (NEW) | 0 | — | co-located tests (real stdin) | — |\n| `tests/export-surface.test.ts` | 29 | `04fb7e6` (2026-07-06) | public-entry surface contract | grows with new exports |\n| `tests/public-api.integration.test.tsx` | 33 | `2d04734` (2026-07-06) | integration via composition root | grows: thread+composer scene |\n| `benchmarks/chat-thread.bench.tsx` (NEW) | 0 | — | mode-matrix thread benchmark | — |\n| `docs/benchmarks/m1-chat-thread-baseline.json` (NEW) | 0 | — | committed M1 baseline | M0 baseline file untouched |\n| `tests/bench-baseline.test.ts` | 83 | `04fb7e6` (2026-07-06) | baseline schema oracle | extends to validate the M1 file too |\n| `examples/chat.tsx` (NEW) | 0 | — | interactive TTFATT demo (thread+composer+streaming) | `examples/basic.tsx` untouched |\n| `package.json` | 67 | `d1a3c0b` (2026-07-06) | manifest + scripts | contract test protects shape; adds `example:chat` script |\n| `tests/package-manifest.test.ts` | 66 | `04fb7e6` (2026-07-06) | manifest contract | gate-scripts assertion grows with `example:chat` — no, script list stays as-is (bench/example not per-variant); no change needed unless script keys asserted change |\n| `CHANGELOG.md` | — | `d1a3c0b` (2026-07-06) | Unreleased empty post-release | every task appends |\n\n### Current callers / dependents\n\n- **Symbol:** `ChatMessage` in `src/chat-message.tsx`\n  - Callers (production): `examples/basic.tsx:3`, `benchmarks/chat-message.bench.tsx` (via\n    `src/index.js`), future `src/chat-thread.tsx`\n  - Callers (tests): `src/chat-message.test.tsx`, `tests/public-api.integration.test.tsx`,\n    `tests/fixtures/no-color-probe.tsx`, `tests/export-surface.test.ts`\n  - External: published API of v0.1.0 — role union EXTENSION is additive (allowed);\n    the invalid-role ERROR MESSAGE text changes (documented in CHANGELOG § Changed)\n- **Symbol:** `defaultTheme`/`TheoTheme`/`TheoThemeOverride` in `src/theme.tsx`\n  - Callers (production): `src/chat-message.tsx:25`; tests as above\n  - Change: additive `role.system` group — existing overrides remain valid\n- **Symbol:** `VERSION`, provider/hook — untouched by M1\n\n### Domain glossary\n\n- **thread message** — `{ id, role, content }` value object; identity (`===`) signals change.\n- **live tail** — the last `windowSize + windowOverscan` messages that re-render (streaming zone).\n- **static prefix** — older messages rendered ONCE through Ink `<Static>` into terminal scrollback.\n- **streaming append-in-place** — caller replaces the LAST message object with a longer `content`.\n- **grapheme** — user-perceived character (`Intl.Segmenter`); cursor ops never split emoji.\n- **submit vs newline** — Enter submits; Ctrl+J (the literal `\\n` byte) inserts newline everywhere;\n  Shift+Enter inserts newline only when the terminal encodes shift (kitty protocol).\n\n### Architecture boundaries affected\n\nPer `rules/architecture.md § 1-3`: `src/text-buffer.ts` is pure domain logic (no ink/react\nimports — unit-testable without TTY); `src/chat-thread.tsx` and `src/chat-composer.tsx` are\ninterface-layer components consuming theme tokens ONLY via `useTheoTheme()` (DIP, same seam as\nM0); `src/index.ts` remains the only public surface. No adapter/external-I/O layer.\n\n## Prior Art & Related Work\n\n- **Internal blueprint:** `knowledge-base/discoveries/blueprints/m1-chat-surface-blueprint.md` —\n  ADRs D1–D6 consumed verbatim (§ ADRs below restates them condensed); Corners 1–4 carry the\n  `file:line` evidence.\n- **Patterns skills:** (none exist).\n- **Reference projects** (read-only clones; key anchors):\n  - `knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/src/primitives/thread/ThreadMessages.tsx:130-166` — windowing mechanics (tailStart, Static prefix, memo tail).\n  - `knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/src/primitives/composer/useTextBuffer.ts:3-26` — flat buffer + grapheme segmenter.\n  - `knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/src/primitives/composer/ComposerInput.tsx:89-236` — keymap + inverse cursor.\n  - `knowledge-base/references/ink/src/components/Static.tsx:21-58` — Static contract (watermark, append-only).\n  - `knowledge-base/references/ink/test/hooks-use-input.tsx` — authoritative stdin byte sequences.\n  - `knowledge-base/references/gemini-cli/packages/cli/src/ui/components/MainContent.tsx:310-320` — production Static split + dynamic pending zone.\n- **External literature:** none beyond the above (all evidence local).\n\n## Objective\n\n- [ ] `ChatMessage` accepts `role=\"system\"` with distinct glyph/color tokens; Blueprint Corner 2 verdict: **ZERO new runtime dependencies**\n> (grapheme handling via built-in `Intl.Segmenter`; ink provides Static/useInput/useFocus).\n\n### Existing — use as-is\n\n| Package | Version | Ecosystem | Why |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `ink` | `^5.2.0` | npm | `Static`, `useInput`, `useFocus`, `Box/Text` — all M1 primitives (Blueprint §\"Coverage Corner 2 — Dependencies\") |\n| `react` | `^18.0.0 \\|\\| ^19.0.0` (peer) | npm | memo/useReducer/useMemo |\n| (devDeps unchanged) | — | npm | vitest/ink-testing-library/tsup/eslint/prettier/tsx as released in v0.1.0 |\n\n### New — to be introduced\n\n| Package | Version | Ecosystem | Rule 9 rationale (libs evaluated) | Why this one |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| (none) | — | — | Evaluated: `string-width` (rejected — Ink wraps text itself at M1), `clipboardy` (rejected — paste is out of M1 scope), `chalk` direct (rejected — ink `<Text inverse>` covers the cursor) | Blueprint Corner 2: analogs' thread/composer code needs no external runtime dep |\n\n### Removed\n\n| Package | Last version | Why removed |\n|---|---|---|\n| (none) | | |\n\n## ADRs\n\n> D1–D6 originate in the blueprint (Blueprint §\"ADRs\" carries the full evidence); restated here\n> condensed and self-contained. D7+ are plan-local.\n\n### D1 — ChatThread: messages-prop API with windowed `<Static>` history (immutable prefix contract)\n\n**Decision:** `ChatThread({ messages, windowSize = 8, windowOverscan = 4 })` with\n`messages: ChatThreadMessage[]` (`{ id: string; role: ChatRole; content: string }`). Prefix\n(`length − windowSize − overscan`, clamped ≥ 0) renders through `<Static>` keyed by `id`; tail\nrenders live via a memoized row. Documented contract: messages that leave the live window are\nFROZEN (append-only history).\n\n**Rationale:** Mirrors the react-ink windowing mechanics with an explicit-props API (M7 owns\nruntime state); gemini-cli proves Static history in production; Ink has no viewport — Static IS\nscroll.\n\n**Alternatives considered:** No windowing by default (rejected: the M1 DoD names `<Static>` for\nhistory); semantic last-user-prompt boundary (rejected: role-policy in a primitive — YAGNI);\nindex keys (rejected: caller data needs stable ids; react-ink can key by index only because its\nstore is append-only).\n\n**Consequences:** Callers own message identity; benchmark data tunes the defaults.\n\n### D2 — Streaming contract: rows memoized by message object identity\n\n**Decision:** Row component wrapped in `memo` with comparator `prev.message === next.message`.\nStreaming = caller replaces the LAST message object with a longer `content`; only that row\nre-renders. No throttling at M1.\n\n**Rationale:** Identity comparison gives the react-ink memo bound without a store; bench\nevidence shows memo+windowing cut mean/peak frame times vs legacy; 16ms token cadence needs no\nthrottle.\n\n**Alternatives considered:** Per-row store subscriptions (rejected: M7); deep-equality memo\n(rejected: O(content) per row per frame; identity IS the React data contract).\n\n**Consequences:** In-place mutation (same reference) does not repaint — documented and tested.\n\n### D3 — ChatComposer: uncontrolled grapheme-aware buffer; Enter submits, Ctrl+J newline\n\n**Decision:** `ChatComposer({ onSubmit, placeholder?, multiLine = true, autoFocus = true })`;\ninternal reducer buffer `{ text, cursorOffset }` with grapheme-aware ops (insert,\ndelete-backward, delete-forward, move-left, move-right, move-home, move-end, newline, clear)\nvia `Intl.Segmenter`. Keymap: printable → insert; Enter → submit (whitespace-only → no-op);\nCtrl+J → newline (reliable everywhere — it IS `\\n`); `key.shift && key.return` → newline when\nthe terminal encodes it; arrows/home/end move; backspace/delete delete. Cursor = `<Text inverse>`\non the grapheme at cursor (space at EOL/newline). Buffer clears on submit.\n\n**Rationale:** Buffer shape + segmenter proven in react-ink `useTextBuffer`; keymap mirrors\n`ComposerInput` with the honest Shift+Enter caveat (ink's `key` cannot distinguish it in legacy\nterminals); Enter-sends is the chat convention (ink-ui evidence).\n\n**Alternatives considered:** Controlled-only value/onChange (rejected at M1: every caller\nre-implements cursor state — controlled mode is additive later); gemini backslash-continuation\n(deferred M2+); word-nav/kill ops (deferred — additive actions); `usePaste` (deferred M2+ —\nseparate event channel).\n\n**Consequences:** Reducer is exported and test-first; TTY-free unit tests cover every op.\n\n### D4 — `system` role via theme tokens (additive extension)\n\n**Decision:** Role union becomes `\"user\" | \"assistant\" | \"system\"` (exported as `ChatRole`);\n`defaultTheme.role.system = { glyph: \"· \", prefix: \"gray\", text: undefined }`;\n`TheoThemeOverride.role.system` accepted; the guard message names the 3-role union.\n\n**Rationale:** M1 DoD names three roles; additive to v0.1.0 API; glyph keeps roles\ndistinguishable in NO_COLOR (M0 review positive finding).\n\n**Alternatives considered:** Separate `SystemMessage` (rejected: M0 D4 chose one role-switched\nprimitive); color-only distinction (rejected: breaks color-independent readability).\n\n**Consequences:** M0's invalid-role test message updates (CHANGELOG § Changed documents it).\n\n### D5 — Test strategy: pure-reducer units + real-stdin integration + rerender streaming sequences\n\n**Decision:** (1) `text-buffer` reducer: TTY-free unit tests per op incl. grapheme edges\n(emoji); (2) composer: real `stdin.write` tests with ink's byte sequences (`\"\\r\"` Enter,\n`\"\u001b[D\"` left-arrow, `\"\b\"`/`\"\"` backspace, `\"\\n\"` Ctrl+J) + one-tick settling;\n(3) streaming: `rerender()` sequences asserting `lastFrame()` progression + a repaint-scope\nunit test (memo spy counting row renders); (4) Static: assert `lastFrame()` contains history +\ntail (Static output accumulates in frames); windowing boundary asserted by counting rendered\nrows.\n\n**Rationale:** Byte sequences are authoritative from ink's own test suite; Static-in-frames\nbehavior verified in ink's tests; mirrors the analogs' idioms with our M0 determinism kit.\n\n**Alternatives considered:** Mocking Static (reserved for boundary unit test if frame-count\nproves flaky); snapshot-only (rejected: interactive semantics need semantic asserts).\n\n**Consequences:** M0 kit (renderFrame, env pins, NO_COLOR subprocess probe) reused; the probe\ngains a thread+system-role scene.\n\n### D6 — Bench: chat-thread mode matrix; NEW baseline file; M0 baseline untouched\n\n**Decision:** `benchmarks/chat-thread.bench.tsx` — 500 messages + 300 streamed tokens\n(replace-last + append pattern), modes `plain` (windowSize ≥ length) vs `windowed` (8+4);\nprotocol/env identical to M0 (1 warmup + 5 runs, mean ± std dev, pinned FORCE_COLOR); output\n`docs/benchmarks/m1-chat-thread-baseline.json` (M0 schema + `mode` field per entry + per-mode\naggregate). `--smoke` = 1 run per mode, no file write.\n\n**Rationale:** Extends the proven harness; plain-vs-windowed is the decision-relevant\ncomparison (rows are memoized by design — a no-memo mode tests nothing we ship);\n`rules/analysis-golden-rule.md § 3` statistical rigor; separate file preserves the M0\nregression anchor.\n\n**Alternatives considered:** Overwrite M0 baseline (rejected: destroys regression anchor);\n3-mode legacy comparison (rejected: legacy mode is not shippable code).\n\n**Consequences:** Windowing benefit is measured, not assumed; defaults revisited with data.\n\n### D7 — ChatThread validates message ids at the boundary (fail-fast on duplicates)\n\n**Decision:** `ChatThread` throws `TypeError` naming the duplicate `id` when `messages`\ncontains duplicate ids (single `Set` pass per render, before any hook side effects beyond the\nscan).\n\n**Rationale:** Duplicate keys inside `<Static>`/React silently corrupt the append-only\nwatermark and row identity — a silent-corruption class bug (`rules/error-handling.md § 1-2`:\nvalidate at the boundary, fail fast with typed errors). O(n) over ids per render is negligible\nagainst Ink's render cost (evidence: M0 bench 11.2ms/frame floor).\n\n**Alternatives considered:** Dev-only check (rejected: the lib has no dev/prod build split at\nM1); silent last-wins dedup (rejected: hides caller bugs — fail-fast rule).\n\n**Consequences:** One more negative-case test; documented in JSDoc.\n\n### D8 — Interactive example degrades to a scripted demo when stdin is not a TTY\n\n**Decision:** `examples/chat.tsx` renders thread + composer with a fake streaming assistant\nreply; when `process.stdin.isTTY` is falsy it skips the composer (renders thread + streaming\nonly) so `pnpm example:chat | cat` stays a valid non-TTY smoke.\n\n**Rationale:** ink's `useInput` requires raw-mode stdin; piped runs must not crash (roadmap\nnon-TTY constraint; M0 example smoke precedent).\n\n**Alternatives considered:** TTY-only example (rejected: loses the CI-viable smoke); mocking\nstdin in the example (rejected: examples are honest consumer code).\n\n**Consequences:** The example doubles as the wiring caller for thread+composer+streaming.\n\n## Drawbacks & Risks\n\n| Drawback / Risk | Severity | Mitigation | Owner |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `<Static>` frozen-prefix contract can surprise callers who edit old messages | Medium | JSDoc contract + a test pinning \"edits to static prefix do not repaint\"; documented in CHANGELOG entry | implement |\n| Shift+Enter newline silently unavailable in legacy terminals (only kitty encodes it) | Medium | Ctrl+J documented as the guaranteed newline; JSDoc caveat (blueprint Rec 8); behavior test asserts Ctrl+J path | implement |\n| Real-stdin tests may flake if settling is insufficient after `stdin.write` | Medium | One-tick `renderFrame` discipline + explicit awaited ticks between writes (ink suite uses staggered timers); two consecutive full-suite runs before commit | implement |\n| ChatThread windowing interacts with ink-testing-library frame accumulation (Static output accumulates) — assertions must target `lastFrame()` content, not frame counts | Low | D5 strategy asserts content presence + row-count via memo spy, not raw frame counts | implement |\n| 500-msg benchmark wall-clock in CI smoke | Low | `--smoke` = 1 run per mode; measured full runs only local (M0 precedent) | implement |\n| Example with fake streaming timer could leave a dangling interval on exit | Low | clear interval on completion + `useApp().exit()` when scripted demo ends | implement |\n\n## Unresolved Questions\n\n(none — every decision is resolved at plan time: blueprint ADRs D1–D6 + plan ADRs D7–D8; the\nonly environment-sensitive behavior, Shift+Enter, ships with the documented Ctrl+J guarantee.)\n\n## Critical paths\n\nFor `/code-quality` D4 when enabled: `src/text-buffer.ts` (cursor/grapheme branching),\n`src/chat-thread.tsx` (window split + duplicate-id guard), `src/chat-composer.tsx` (keymap\ndispatch).\n\n## Dependency Graph\n\n```\nPhase 1 (system role) ──▶ Phase 2 (ChatThread) ──▶ Phase 3 (ChatComposer) ──▶ Phase 4 (bench + example)\n                                                                                     │\n                                                                                     ▼\n                                                                       Final Phase (integration validation)\n```\n\nSequential: thread rows render ChatMessage (needs system role); the example composes\nthread+composer; bench renders the thread. Composer's reducer (T3.1) is independent of Phase 2\nbut kept in sequence for one-vertical-slice discipline.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 1: System role\n\n**Objective:** `ChatMessage` accepts `system`; tokens + guard + tests updated.\n\n### T1.1 — Extend role union + system tokens + guard message\n\n#### Objective\n`ChatRole = \"user\" | \"assistant\" | \"system\"` exported; `defaultTheme.role.system` tokens;\nguard message names the 3-role union; existing tests updated.\n\n#### Why this step (action + reasoning)\n\n1. **What:** RED tests for the system-role render + updated guard message + theme tokens; then\n   the minimal union/token change.\n2. **Why now:** Phase 2's thread rows must render all three roles; the change is additive to\n   the public API and every later phase depends on it (D4).\n\n#### Evidence\n- Blueprint §\"D4\" (glyph `\"· \"`, gray prefix); M0 review positive finding (glyph keeps\n  NO_COLOR readability); `knowledge-base/references/gemini-cli/packages/cli/src/ui/components/messages/UserMessage.tsx:26` (glyph idiom precedent).\n\n#### Files to edit\n```\nsrc/theme.tsx              — role.system tokens in TheoTheme/defaultTheme/TheoThemeOverride\nsrc/theme.test.tsx         — RED: system tokens present + overridable\nsrc/chat-message.tsx       — role union + exported ChatRole + guard message update\nsrc/chat-message.test.tsx  — RED: system render + updated invalid-role message (\"model\" now invalid)\nsrc/index.ts               — export type ChatRole\ntests/export-surface.test.ts — extend: defaultTheme.role.system.glyph asserted\nCHANGELOG.md               — Added (system role) + Changed (guard message text)\n```\n\n#### Deep file dependency analysis\n- `src/theme.tsx`: `TheoTheme.role` gains `system: RoleTokens`; merge function gains the leaf\n  spread for system (same shape as user/assistant — Baseline row). Downstream:\n  `chat-message.tsx` reads `theme.role[role]`; `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` stays total because\n  the union and the record keys stay in sync.\n- `src/chat-message.tsx`: guard condition adds `role !== \"system\"`; message text updates.\n  Downstream: M0 test asserting the old message updates HERE (documented).\n- `src/index.ts`: `export type { ChatRole }`.\n\n#### Deep Dives\n- Tokens: `system: { glyph: \"· \", prefix: \"gray\", text: undefined }` — dim-gray affordance,\n  distinguishable in NO_COLOR by the `·` glyph.\n- Edge cases: system role under NO_COLOR (covered by the probe extension in T4.2);\n  override of only `role.system.glyph` preserves sibling leaves (existing leaf-merge tests\n  extended by one case).\n\n#### Tasks\n1. RED tests (theme + chat-message) — fail on missing tokens/union\n2. GREEN minimal (union + tokens + guard text)\n3. Extend export-surface test; CHANGELOG entries\n\n#### TDD\n```\nRED:     renders_system_message_with_glyph_prefix() — const frame = await renderFrame(<ChatMessage role=\"system\">note</ChatMessage>); expect(frame).toContain(\"·\"); expect(frame).toContain(\"note\")\nRED:     system_frame_matches_snapshot_under_forced_color() — expect(frame).toMatchSnapshot(\"chat-message-system\") inside <Box width={40}>\nRED:     default_theme_exposes_system_tokens() — expect(defaultTheme.role.system.glyph).toBe(\"· \"); expect(defaultTheme.role.system.prefix).toBe(\"gray\")\nRED:     system_tokens_overridable_via_provider() — provider theme={{ role: { system: { glyph: \"§ \" } } }}; expect(frame).toContain(\"§\")\nRED:     invalid_role_message_names_three_role_union() — expect(() => ChatMessage({ role: \"model\" as never, children: \"x\" })).toThrow('ChatMessage: invalid role \"model\" — expected \"user\" | \"assistant\" | \"system\"')\nGREEN:   Extend union + tokens + guard until all pass (existing 2-role tests stay green)\nREFACTOR: None expected\nVERIFY:  pnpm vitest run src/theme.test.tsx src/chat-message.test.tsx\n```\n\n#### Concurrency tests\n\n(none — single-threaded)\n\n#### Acceptance Criteria\n- [ ] `pnpm vitest run src/theme.test.tsx src/chat-message.test.tsx` exits 0 (all roles)\n- [ ] `pnpm typecheck` exits 0 — `ChatRole` exported, record/union in sync\n- [ ] Pass: quality — `pnpm lint` exits 0; `wc -l src/theme.tsx` <= 500\n- [ ] CHANGELOG updated — `grep -q \"system\" CHANGELOG.md` exits 0\n\n#### DoD (Definition of Done)\n- [ ] `pnpm gates` exits 0\n- [ ] CHANGELOG updated (Added + Changed)\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2: ChatThread\n\n**Objective:** Windowed Static history + memoized live tail with the streaming contract proven.\n\n### T2.1 — ChatThread component (window split + memo rows + duplicate-id guard)\n\n#### Objective\n`src/chat-thread.tsx`: `ChatThreadMessage`/`ChatThreadProps` types + `ChatThread` with\nStatic prefix, memo tail, D7 guard; exported from the entry.\n\n#### Why this step (action + reasoning)\n\n1. **What:** RED tests (render both zones, window boundary row-count, duplicate-id typed error,\n   empty list) then the minimal component per D1/D7.\n2. **Why now:** The thread is the milestone's centerpiece; composer and bench build on it.\n\n#### Evidence\n- Windowing mechanics: `knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/src/primitives/thread/ThreadMessages.tsx:130-166`.\n- Static contract: `knowledge-base/references/ink/src/components/Static.tsx:21-58`.\n- Memo comparator precedent: `knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/src/primitives/internal/MemoMessage.tsx:31-35` (ours simplifies to message identity — Blueprint §\"D2\").\n\n#### Files to edit\n```\nsrc/chat-thread.test.tsx — (NEW) RED suite\nsrc/chat-thread.tsx      — (NEW) component + types\nsrc/index.ts             — export ChatThread + types\ntests/export-surface.test.ts — extend\nCHANGELOG.md             — Added entry\n```\n\n#### Deep file dependency analysis\n- `src/chat-thread.tsx`: imports `Static, Box` from ink, `ChatMessage`+`ChatRole` from\n  `./chat-message.js` (interface layer composing the M0 primitive), `memo/useMemo` from react.\n  No theme import (rows delegate to ChatMessage — DIP preserved).\n- `src/index.ts`: gains `ChatThread`, `ChatThreadMessage`, `ChatThreadProps`.\n\n#### Deep Dives\n- Signature:\n\n```pseudocode\ninterface ChatThreadMessage { id: string; role: ChatRole; content: string }\ninterface ChatThreadProps { messages: ChatThreadMessage[]; windowSize?: number; windowOverscan?: number }\nChatThread({ messages, windowSize = 8, windowOverscan = 4 }):\n  assertUniqueIds(messages)                       -- D7: TypeError naming the duplicate id\n  tailStart = max(0, messages.length - max(0,windowSize) - max(0,windowOverscan))\n  prefix = messages.slice(0, tailStart)           -- <Static items={prefix}> keyed by m.id\n  tail   = messages.slice(tailStart)              -- <MemoRow key={m.id} message={m}>\n  render <>{Static when prefix.length>0}{<Box flexDirection=\"column\">tail rows</Box>}</>\n\nMemoRow = memo(({message}) => <ChatMessage role={message.role}>{message.content}</ChatMessage>,\n               (prev, next) => prev.message === next.message)\n```\n\n- Invariants: prefix rows are appended-only (Static watermark — never re-rendered); tail rows\n  re-render ONLY when their message object identity changes (D2); empty `messages` renders\n  nothing (no Static mount — avoids the ink#904 class of issues).\n- Edge cases: `windowSize=0` (everything except overscan tail in Static); thread shorter than\n  window (no Static at all); duplicate ids (TypeError); replace-last streaming (only last row\n  repaints — proven by memo spy test in T2.2).\n\n#### Tasks\n1. RED suite (7 tests below)\n2. GREEN minimal component\n3. then the pure reducer.\n2. **Why now:** The reducer is the composer's core logic; TTY-free tests give the tightest TDD\n   loop and 100% critical-path coverage before any rendering exists (D3/D5).\n\n#### Evidence\n- Buffer shape + segmenter: `knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/src/primitives/composer/useTextBuffer.ts:3-26, 31-34, 137-309`.\n\n#### Files to edit\n```\nsrc/text-buffer.test.ts — (NEW) RED suite\nsrc/text-buffer.ts      — (NEW) reducer\nCHANGELOG.md            — Added entry (grouped with T3.2)\n```\n\n#### Deep Dives\n- State `{ text: string; cursorOffset: number }`; actions: `insert{text}`, `delete-backward`,\n  `delete-forward`, `move-left`, `move-right`, `move-home`, `move-end`, `newline`, `clear`.\n- Grapheme discipline: `Intl.Segmenter(undefined, { granularity: \"grapheme\" })`; move/delete\n  step by grapheme (emoji \"👍\" is ONE step); home/end = line boundaries within multi-line text\n  (previous/next `\\n`), matching the analog's multiline semantics.\n- Edge cases: cursor at 0 (delete-backward no-op), cursor at end (delete-forward no-op),\n  insert at middle, newline at middle, empty text ops, emoji at cursor boundary.\n\n#### Pseudo-code / Signatures\n```pseudocode\nfunction textBufferReducer(state, action): TextBufferState\n  case insert:  text.slice(0,cur) + action.text + text.slice(cur); cur += action.text.length\n  case delete-backward: prevGrapheme(cur) → splice out; cur = prevStart\n  case move-left/right: cur = prev/nextGraphemeBoundary\n  case move-home/end:   cur = lineStart/lineEnd (nearest \"\\n\" boundaries)\n  case newline: insert \"\\n\"\n  case clear:   { text: \"\", cursorOffset: 0 }\n\n# Example\ninput:  {text:\"héllo\", cursorOffset:5} + move-left ×2\noutput: cursorOffset lands before \"l\" (grapheme steps, é = one step)\n```\n\n#### Tasks\n1. prefix indices → `<Static>`; tail wrapped\n1047\t  in `MemoMessage` keyed by index —\n1048\t  `.claude/knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/src/primitives/thread/ThreadMessages.tsx:130-166`.\n1049\t- Memo wrapper: `memo(Impl, (prev,next) => prev.index === next.index && Object.is(prev.render, next.render))` —\n1050\t  `.claude/knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/src/primitives/internal/MemoMessage.tsx:31-35`.\n1051\t- Streaming mutation pattern for the workload (grow last assistant msg + append placeholder)\n1052\t  — `.claude/knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/benchmarks/long-thread.bench.tsx:244-259`;\n1053\t  3-mode trial driver + attribution deltas `:288-342`.\n1054\t- **M1 bench design:** extend OUR harness with `benchmarks/chat-thread.bench.tsx` — mode\n1055\t  matrix {plain, windowed} × 500 messages × 300 streamed tokens, same metrics\n1056\t  (frames, mean/peak ms-per-frame, ±std dev, pinned color env) and a NEW baseline JSON\n1057\t  `docs/benchmarks/m1-chat-thread-baseline.json` (same schema + `mode` field per entry).\n1058\t  The M0 chat-message baseline stays untouched (regression comparability).\n1059\t\n1060\t---\n1061\t\n1062\t## Coverage Corner 4 — Techniques\n1063\t\n1064\t*(Answers Q1, Q2, Q3.)*\n1065\t\n1066\t### Q1 — ChatThread composition\n1067\t\n1068\t- react-ink: optional windowing; prefix→`<Static>`, tail→live `MemoMessage[]`; overscan\n1069\t  default 4 — `ThreadMessages.tsx:35-49, 130-166` (quotes in Corner 3).\n1070\t- gemini-cli: Static holds header + history up to & incl. the LAST USER PROMPT; Static\n1079\t  IS the scroll mechanism (plan ADR D2 confirmed — bubbles not needed).\n1080\t\n1081\t### Q2 — Streaming render without flicker\n1082\t\n1083\t- Per-message subscription (only the growing row recomputes) + memo boundary + windowing\n1084\t  bound the reconciliation scope; the growing message NEVER enters Static —\n1085\t  `long-thread.bench.tsx:99-109` (per-row length subscriber), `:112-128` (legacy — whole array\n1086\t  reconciles), `MemoMessage.tsx:31-35`, `MainContent.tsx:320` (pendingItems outside Static).\n1087\t- No throttle/re-key needed at 16ms token cadence — Ink flushes between ticks\n1088\t  (`long-thread.bench.tsx:152-155`).\n1089\t\n1090\t### Q3 — ChatComposer input\n1091\t\n1092\t- Buffer: flat `{ text, cursorOffset, preferredColumn }` + reducer actions (insert/delete/\n1093\t  move/kill/word ops), grapheme-aware via `Intl.Segmenter` —\n1094\t  `.claude/knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/src/primitives/composer/useTextBuffer.ts:3-26, 31-34, 137-309`.\n1095\t- Key mapping (ComposerInput): Ctrl+J always newline (multiLine); Return → newline when\n1096\t  `multiLine && (!submitOnEnter || key.shift)` else submit; emacs bindings; arrows; cursor\n1097\t  rendered as `<Text inverse>{graphemeAtCursor}</Text>` (space when at newline/EOL) —\n1098\t  `.claude/knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/src/primitives/composer/ComposerInput.tsx:89-236`.\n1099\t- gemini-cli: dedicated NEWLINE key + SUBMIT with backslash-continuation (`\\` before cursor →\n1100\t  newline instead of submit) + 40ms paste-protection —\n1101\t  `.claude/knowledge-base/references/gemini-cli/packages/cli/src/ui/components/InputPrompt.tsx:1074-1077, 1258-1283`.\n1102\t- ink `useInput` `key` fields: return/escape/ctrl/shift/tab/meta/arrows/backspace/delete\n1103\t  (+kitty-only extras). **Shift+Enter is NOT a distinct key** — both set `key.return`;\n1104\t  `key.shift` only arrives when the terminal actually encodes it (kitty protocol) —\n1105\t  `.claude/knowledge-base/references/ink/src/hooks/use-input.ts:9-124`. Paste arrives via the\n1106\t  separate `usePaste` channel (bracketed paste) —\n1107\t  `.claude/knowledge-base/references/ink/src/hooks/use-paste.ts:14-80`.\n1108\t- ink-ui contrast (single-line): `key.return` always submits; 4-action reducer; chalk-inverse\n1109\t  cursor — `.claude/knowledge-base/references/ink-ui/source/components/text-input/use-text-input.ts:34-115`.\n1110\t\n1111\t## Cross-cutting Comparison\n1112\t\n1113\t| Dimension | react-ink | gemini-cli | ink | ink-ui |\n1114\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n1115\t| History freeze | windowed prefix → Static (`ThreadMessages.tsx:157-166`) | Static up to last user prompt (`MainContent.tsx:144-152`) | Static = append-only scrollback (`Static.tsx:28-38`) | n/a |\n1116\t| Streaming zone | live tail w/ memo rows | pendingItems below Static (`MainContent.tsx:320`) | dynamic output region | n/a |\n1117\t| Input buffer | flat string+offset, Intl.Segmenter (`useTextBuffer.ts`) | lines[]+[row,col]+visual map (`text-buffer.ts:3913-4010`) | — | flat, 4 actions |\n1118\t| Submit vs newline | Enter submits; Shift+Enter/Ctrl+J newline (`ComposerInput.tsx:147-158`) | SUBMIT key + `\\`-continuation (`InputPrompt.tsx:1275-1279`) | key.return only (`use-input.ts`) | Enter always submits |\n1119\t| Input tests | direct handler invocation | stdin.write + act/waitFor | stdin byte suite | stdin.write + delay |\n1120\t\n1121\t## ADRs\n1122\t\n1123\t### D1 — ChatThread: messages-prop API with windowed `<Static>` history (immutable prefix contract)\n1124\t\n1125\t**Decision:** `ChatThread({ messages, windowSize = 8, windowOverscan = 4, renderMessage? })`\n1126\twhere `messages: ChatThreadMessage[]` (`{ id, role, content }`). Prefix\n1127\t(`len - windowSize - overscan`) renders through `<Static>` keyed by message id; tail renders\n1128\tlive, each row wrapped in a memoized row component. Documented contract: **messages that\n1129\tleave the live window are frozen** (append-only history — edits to them will not repaint).\n1130\t\n1131\t**Rationale:** Mirrors `ThreadMessages.tsx:130-166` mechanics with an explicit-props API (no\n1132\truntime store at M1 — plan ADR D3); gemini-cli proves Static-history in production; Ink has\n1133\tno viewport — Static IS scroll (`Static.tsx:21-27`).\n1134\t\n1135\t**Alternatives considered:** No windowing by default like react-ink (rejected: roadmap DoD\n1136\tnames `<Static>` for history — a default that never uses Static fails the DoD); semantic\n1137\tboundary at last-user-prompt like gemini-cli (rejected: requires role semantics in the\n1138\tcomponent — caller-specific policy, YAGNI at M1); id-less index keys (rejected: react keys on\n1139\treorder-able data need stable ids; react-ink keys by index only because its store guarantees\n1140\tappend-only).\n1141\t\n1142\t**Consequences:** Callers own message identity (`id`); M7's adapter will emit stable ids.\n1143\tWindow defaults tuned by the M1 benchmark data.\n1144\t\n1145\t### D2 — Streaming contract: memoized rows by message identity; growing message stays in the live tail\n1146\t\n1147\t**Decision:** Row memo comparator: `prev.message === next.message` (object identity) — the\n1148\tcaller streams by replacing the LAST message object (`[...msgs.slice(0,-1), {...last,\n1149\tcontent: last.content + token}]`); only that row re-renders. No throttle at M1.\n1150\t\n1151\t**Rationale:** Simpler than react-ink's render-fn memo (`MemoMessage.tsx:31-35`) because our\n1152\tAPI passes message objects, not store accessors; identity comparison gives the same bound\n1153\t(only-changed-row reconciles). Bench evidence: memo/windowed modes cut mean+peak frame times\n1154\tvs legacy (`long-thread.bench.tsx:288-342`); 16ms cadence needs no throttle (`:152-155`).\n1155\t\n1156\t**Alternatives considered:** Per-row store subscription à la react-ink (rejected: M7 owns\n1157\tstate); deep-equality memo (rejected: O(content) per row per frame — identity is the\n1158\tcontract).\n1159\t\n1160\t**Consequences:** Mutating a message in place (same reference) will NOT repaint — documented\n1161\t+ tested. This is the standard React data contract.\n1162\t\n1163\t### D3 — ChatComposer: uncontrolled grapheme-aware buffer; Enter submits, Ctrl+J newline (Shift+Enter honored when the terminal encodes it)\n1164\t\n1165\t**Decision:** `ChatComposer({ onSubmit, placeholder?, multiLine = true, autoFocus = true })`\n1166\twith an internal reducer buffer `{ text, cursorOffset }` (Intl.Segmenter graphemes; insert/\n1167\tdelete-backward/delete-forward/move-left/right/home/end + newline insert). Keymap: printable →\n1168\tinsert; Enter → submit (trimmed-empty guard: no-op); **Ctrl+J → newline** (reliable in every\n1169\tterminal — it IS the `\\n` byte); `key.shift && key.return` → newline when the terminal\n1170\tencodes it (kitty); arrows/home/end move; backspace/delete delete. Cursor = `<Text inverse>`\n1171\ton the grapheme at cursor (space at EOL/newline). Multi-line renders as wrapped lines; on\n1172\tsubmit the buffer clears.\n1173\t\n1174\t**Rationale:** Buffer shape + grapheme discipline proven in `useTextBuffer.ts:3-26,31-34`;\n1175\tkeymap mirrors `ComposerInput.tsx:89-158` with the honest Shift+Enter caveat from\n1176\t`use-input.ts` (no distinct encoding in legacy terminals). ink-ui shows Enter-always-submit\n1177\tis the single-line convention — we keep it as the multi-line default too (chat UX: Enter\n1178\tsends), with Ctrl+J as the guaranteed newline path.\n1179\t\n1180\t**Alternatives considered:** Controlled-only `value/onChange` (rejected at M1: forces every\n1181\tcaller to re-implement cursor state; add controlled mode when a consumer needs it — YAGNI);\n1182\tgemini's backslash-continuation (deferred: nice-to-have, M2+); full emacs kill/word ops\n1183\t(deferred: additive later; M1 ships core ops only); `usePaste` integration (deferred to M2+ —\n1184\tseparate channel per `use-paste.ts:17-18`).\n1185\t\n1186\t**Consequences:** Word-nav/kill ops and paste land later as additive actions; the reducer is\n1187\texported test-first so ops are unit-testable without a TTY.\n1188\t\n1189\t### D4 — ChatMessage gains `system` role via theme tokens (breaking-safe extension)\n1190\t\n1191\t**Decision:** Extend the role union to `\"user\" | \"assistant\" | \"system\"`; add\n1192\t`defaultTheme.role.system` tokens (glyph `\"· \"`, prefix color `\"gray\"`, text undefined);\n1193\t`TheoThemeOverride.role.system` accepted; the EC-1 guard message updates to name the 3-role\n1194\tunion.\n1195\t\n1196\t**Rationale:** Roadmap M1 DoD names three roles. Purely additive to the M0 API (existing\n1197\tconsumers unaffected); token-driven per ADR D5 (M0) so NO_COLOR keeps role\n1198\tdistinguishability via glyphs (M0 review positive finding).\n1199\t\n1200\t**Alternatives considered:** Separate `SystemMessage` component (rejected: M0 ADR D4 chose a\n1201\tsingle role-switched primitive); color-only distinction (rejected: breaks the color-independent\n1202\ta11y property).\n1203\t\n1204\t**Consequences:** M0's invalid-role test asserting the 2-role message updates (documented\n1205\ttest change, not silent).\n1206\t\n1207\t### D5 — Test strategy: real-stdin integration for composer; rerender-sequence for streaming; lastFrame for Static\n1208\t\n1209\t**Decision:** (1) Composer: reducer unit tests (pure, no TTY) + real `stdin.write` tests\n1210\tusing the ink-suite byte sequences (`\"\\r\"`, `\"\u001b[D\"`, `\"\b\"`, `\"\\n\"` for Ctrl+J) with\n1211\tone-tick settling; (2) streaming: drive `rerender()` with growing content; assert\n1212\t`lastFrame()` progression + a row-repaint-scope unit test via memo spy; (3) ChatThread/Static:\n1213\tassert `lastFrame()` contains history + live tail (Static output accumulates in frames per\n1214\t`ink/test/components.tsx:494-512`); windowing boundary unit-tested by counting rendered rows\n1215\t(react-ink mock-Static idiom when needed).\n1216\t\n1217\t**Rationale:** Byte sequences are authoritative from ink's own suite (Q4); Static-in-frames\n1218\tbehavior verified (Q5); action-level tests mirror `ComposerInput.test.tsx:105-127`.\n1219\t\n1220\t**Alternatives considered:** Mock-everything à la gemini (rejected: our components have no\n1221\tservice deps — real stdin is cheaper and more honest); snapshot-only (rejected: interactive\n1222\tbehavior needs semantic asserts).\n1223\t\n1224\t**Consequences:** M0 determinism kit (renderFrame, FORCE_COLOR pins, NO_COLOR subprocess\n1225\tprobe) is reused as-is; the NO_COLOR probe gains a thread+composer scene.\n1226\t\n1227\t### D6 — Bench extension: chat-thread bench with mode matrix; new baseline JSON; M0 baseline untouched\n1228\t\n1229\t**Decision:** `benchmarks/chat-thread.bench.tsx`: 500 messages, 300 streamed tokens, modes\n1230\t{plain-column, windowed(8+4)}; separate file preserves M0 regression comparability.\n1236\t\n1237\t**Alternatives considered:** Overwrite the M0 baseline (rejected: destroys the regression\n1238\tanchor); add legacy/no-memo mode (rejected: our rows are memoized by design — plain vs\n1239\twindowed is the decision-relevant comparison).\n1240\t\n1241\t**Consequences:** Real windowing-vs-plain data lands in the repo;
